## III<sub>.</sub> P<sub>reliminaries</sub>

## A. Principles of 3D Gaussian Splatting

3DGS [2] ex<sub>p</sub>licitl<sub>y</sub> re<sub>p</sub>resents a scene usin<sub>g</sub> a collection of 3D G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an</sub> k<sub>erne</sub>l<sub>s, o</sub>f<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng super</sub>i<sub>or compu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ency an</sub>d inter<sub>p</sub>retabilit<sub>y</sub> com<sub>p</sub>ared to im<sub>p</sub>licit models like NeRF [1]. Each G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> i<sub>s parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d b<sub>y a cen</sub>t<sub>er pos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> $\pmb { \mu } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { 3 }$ <sub>a</sub> <sub>spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x</sub> $\Sigma \in \mathbb { R } ^ { 3 \times 3 }$ , <sup>an</sup> <sup>o</sup>p<sup>acit</sup>y $o \in \mathbb { R }$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d a color vector c encoded via SH coeficients. In practice, the covariance matrix Σ is decomposed into a scaling matrix $S$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> rotation matrix R to ensure positive semi-definiteness, expressed as $\Sigma = R S S ^ { \top } R ^ { \top }$ . For rendering, each 3D Gaussian is projected <sub>on</sub>t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 2D i<sub>mage</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ane,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>we</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>y</sub> $\mu _ { i } ^ { p }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\Sigma _ { i } ^ { p }$ th<sub>e</sub> <sub>cen</sub>t<sub>er</sub> (mean) and covariance of its 2D footprint for the i-th primitive. Th<sub>e per-p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l <sub>opac</sub>it<sub>y con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f thi<sub>s pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ve a</sub>t <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l $p$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

$$
\alpha _ { i } ( p ) = o _ { i } \exp \left( - \frac { 1 } { 2 } ( p - \pmb { \mu } _ { i } ^ { p } ) ^ { \top } ( \Sigma _ { i } ^ { p } ) ^ { - 1 } ( p - \pmb { \mu } _ { i } ^ { p } ) \right)\tag{1}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l <sub>co</sub>l<sub>or</sub> $C ( \boldsymbol p )$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>en compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a a</sub>l<sub>p</sub>h<sub>a-</sub>bl<sub>en</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e sor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ves:</sub>

$$
C ( \boldsymbol { p } ) = \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } c _ { i } \alpha _ { i } ( \boldsymbol { p } ) \prod _ { j = 1 } ^ { i - 1 } \bigl ( 1 - \alpha _ { j } ( \boldsymbol { p } ) \bigr ) .\tag{2}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\alpha _ { i }$ represents the opacity of the i-th Gaussian after projection.

## B<sub>.</sub> D<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>c</sub> S<sub>cene</sub> R<sub>epresen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d St<sub>orage</sub> Ch<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>enges</sub>

T<sub>o ex</sub>t<sub>e</sub>nd 3DGS t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>y</sub>n<sub>a</sub>mi<sub>c sce</sub>n<sub>es,</sub> 4DGS fr<sub>a</sub>m<sub>ewo</sub>rk<sub>s</sub> t<sub>yp-</sub> i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ves</sub> i<sub>n a canon</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>space</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>un</sub>d<sub>ergo</sub> ti<sub>me-</sub> <sub>vary</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ves</sub> i<sub>n wor</sub>ld <sub>space.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y, a canon</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an</sub> $G$ i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> $G ^ { \prime }$ at time t via a learned deformation $\Delta G \colon$

$$
G ^ { \prime } = G + \Delta G , \quad \mathrm { w h e r e } \quad \Delta G = \Phi ( f , t ) .\tag{3}
$$

To capture the local geometry surrounding the primitive, we <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e e</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>corner ver</sub>ti<sub>ces</sub> $\tilde { \pmb { \mu } }$ <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e enc</sub>l<sub>os</sub>i<sub>ng voxe</sub>l b<sub>y</sub> applying binary ofsets to v:
